Each nightly snapshot adds one row of predictions to the on-disk archive. A prediction becomes scorable once its horizon (30 or 60 trading days) has elapsed — the 30-day hit rates appear first, the 60-day follow.

The 30-day horizon is shown by default — that's the swing trader's primary frame. The longer horizon (a confirmation frame) is collapsed; click to expand. Verdicts unqualified by user apply to both Aidy and Jesse (they share the watchlist universe); a per-user qualifier appears only when they've diverged. Definitions of each label are in the glossary below.
What do ENTER / WAIT / HOLD / TRIM / SKIP / EXIT mean?
ENTER
Open a new position. Conviction is above the ENTER threshold AND no Layer-3 veto fires on this horizon. The price-levels panel above each card shows the suggested entry zone.
WAIT
Don't enter today, but don't dismiss the ticker either. Either (a) conviction is between the WAIT floor and the ENTER threshold, or (b) a Layer-3 veto (catalyst inside horizon, downtrend regime) is temporarily blocking ENTER. Re-evaluates each night.
SKIP
Don't enter — conviction is below the WAIT floor OR a hard veto (e.g. fair-value extreme, Tier-C illiquidity) fires. Re-evaluates each night but the bar to flip back to actionable is higher than for WAIT.
HOLD
Keep the position. Conviction is above the WAIT floor and the original thesis is intact. Default state for entered positions on a quiet, in-range day.
TRIM
Take partial profits — sell some of the position but not all. Triggered when fair-value premium goes ≥2σ above the engine's fair-value mean OR the regime flips from positive to negative on an entered position. Reduce exposure, don't pre-commit to a full exit.
EXIT
Close the entire position. Triggered when conviction falls below the WAIT floor (thesis fully weakened), the stop level is breached, OR the engine deems the trade structurally broken (regime collapse, fair-value catastrophe).

Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 43% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 43% confidence
Days in this regime61 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)
PL is trending up but with elevated daily swings (43% confidence). Nearest alternative is uptrend_quiet at 23% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.

How wild are the daily moves? 103% realized · 30d forecast 103% (→ flat)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)103.1% annualized≈ 6.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d102.6% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d101.1% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $46.46 vs FV mean $27.00 (+3.13σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$2.14 — $27.00 — $27.00
Current market price$46.46+3.13σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you ≥ +2σ premium — Layer-3 fair-value veto fires: watching → SKIP, entered → TRIM (take some profit at this extreme).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

How wild are the daily moves? 348% realized · 30d forecast 274% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)348.3% annualized≈ 21.9% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d273.6% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d243.4% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $1.78 vs FV mean $4.00 (-3.70σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$2.80 — $4.00 — $5.20
Current market price$1.78-3.70σ vs FV mean

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.

How wild are the daily moves? 154% realized · 30d forecast 137% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).

How wild are the daily moves? 151% realized · 30d forecast 129% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)151.1% annualized≈ 9.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d129.3% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d118.0% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $30.11 vs FV mean $6.82 (+11.42σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$0.84 — $6.82 — $9.00
Current market price$30.11+11.42σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you ≥ +2σ premium — Layer-3 fair-value veto fires: watching → SKIP, entered → TRIM (take some profit at this extreme).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist C → measured C (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 43% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.

How wild are the daily moves? 111% realized · 30d forecast 108% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)111.4% annualized≈ 7.0% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d107.7% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d101.1% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $122.39 vs FV mean $99.00 (+1.58σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$69.30 — $99.00 — $128.70

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 43% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 43% confidence
Days in this regime61 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)

Nearest alternative is uptrend_quiet at 25% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.

How wild are the daily moves? 102% realized · 30d forecast 93% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)101.8% annualized≈ 6.4% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d92.5% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d88.9% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $905.00 vs FV mean $1000.00 (-0.47σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$197.37 — $1000.00 — $1000.00
Current market price$905.00-0.47σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Fairly valued — no valuation-driven push in either direction.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 44% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 44% confidence

Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+18.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)

How wild are the daily moves? 86% realized · 30d forecast 78% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)86.3% annualized≈ 5.4% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d78.3% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d75.2% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $109.33 vs FV mean $82.00 (+2.64σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$40.54 — $82.00 — $82.00
Current market price$109.33+2.64σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you ≥ +2σ premium — Layer-3 fair-value veto fires: watching → SKIP, entered → TRIM (take some profit at this extreme).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.

How wild are the daily moves? 57% realized · 30d forecast 77% (↑ rising)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)57.2% annualized≈ 3.6% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d77.1% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d83.9% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)wideTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to rise — choppier days ahead. Stops and targets should be set wider to avoid being shaken out by normal noise. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $127.42 vs FV mean $131.00 (-0.18σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$91.70 — $131.00 — $170.30
Current market price$127.42-0.18σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingAnalyst PT consensus
What this means for you Fairly valued — no valuation-driven push in either direction.
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist C → measured B (looser)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? crisis · 50% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state crisis — Extreme volatility, breakdown of normal regime structure — typical of macro shocks or company-specific catastrophes. 50% confidence
Days in this regime23 sessionslonger = more durable
Implied annual drift (fed to MC)+0.0% per yearthis number directly shifts P(target)
IPGP is in a high-volatility breakdown regime (50% confidence). Observed features over last 60 sessions: trend -24.6%/yr implied, realized vol 87%/yr, vol-of-vol 32%. 23 sessions in this regime — persistent. Nearest alternative is downtrend at 26% — the call is at a boundary, treat with care.
What this means for you Drift fed to MC reflects regime direction. Watch for confidence ≥ 70% if state is downtrend/crisis — that threshold triggers the Layer-3 ENTER veto.
How wild are the daily moves? 87% realized · 30d forecast 75% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)86.7% annualized≈ 5.5% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d74.7% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d70.6% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $115.16 vs FV mean $158.00 (-1.74σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$59.61 — $158.00 — $158.00
Current market price$115.16-1.74σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). How wild are the daily moves? 63% realized · 30d forecast 62% (→ flat)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)62.7% annualized≈ 3.9% typical daily move

Forecast end-of-60d62.0% annualized

What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $921.26 vs FV mean $338.91 (+3.33σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$33.95 — $338.91 — $733.50
Current market price$921.26+3.33σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you ≥ +2σ premium — Layer-3 fair-value veto fires: watching → SKIP, entered → TRIM (take some profit at this extreme).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 39% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 39% confidence

How wild are the daily moves? 50% realized · 30d forecast 48% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)50.5% annualized≈ 3.2% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d47.8% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d47.1% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $458.17 vs FV mean $520.00 (-0.44σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$114.55 — $520.00 — $680.35

Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist B → measured B (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? downtrend · 43% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state downtrend — Sustained downward trend with persistent selling pressure — historically these don't reverse quickly. 43% confidence

How wild are the daily moves? 76% realized · 30d forecast 67% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)75.7% annualized≈ 4.8% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d66.5% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d60.2% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $135.86 vs FV mean $156.22 (-3.32σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$135.00 — $156.22 — $159.54
Current market price$135.86-3.32σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured C (stricter)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_noisy · 37% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_noisy — Trending upward but with elevated daily volatility — the direction is right but the path is choppy. 37% confidence

How wild are the daily moves? 37% realized · 30d forecast 34% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)36.9% annualized≈ 2.3% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d33.9% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d32.6% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast to fall meaningfully — calmer days expected. Tighter stops can be justified. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $369.05 vs FV mean $145.66 (+13.72σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$131.00 — $145.66 — $196.12
Current market price$369.05+13.72σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingDCF, Forward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you ≥ +2σ premium — Layer-3 fair-value veto fires: watching → SKIP, entered → TRIM (take some profit at this extreme).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ✓ watchlist B → measured B (match)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Market mood — is the stock trending or choppy? uptrend_quiet · 40% confidence
What this is A statistical model classifies the past ~60 sessions into one of five regimes (uptrend-quiet, uptrend-noisy, downtrend, sideways, crisis). The current regime + its confidence drive (1) the daily drift parameter inside Monte Carlo (Layer 1 of conviction) and (2) the Layer-3 ENTER veto when state is downtrend/crisis with ≥70% confidence.
Current state uptrend_quiet — Sustained upward trend with normal day-to-day price swings — the calmest, most predictable bullish regime. 40% confidence
Days in this regime8 sessionslonger = more durable

How wild are the daily moves? 27% realized · 30d forecast 25% (↓ falling)
What this is Realized volatility = the standard deviation of daily returns, scaled to an annual figure (multiply by √252). A 30% annualized vol means the stock's typical year-over-year price swing is about 30% of its own price. GARCH(1,1) is a model that captures how today's vol depends on recent vol (vol tends to cluster — calm follows calm, wild follows wild).
Current realized vol (60d trailing)26.7% annualized≈ 1.7% typical daily move
Forecast end-of-30d25.1% annualized
Forecast end-of-60d23.4% annualized
Confidence band (set by tier)moderateTier A tight, B moderate, C wide
What this means for you Vol forecast roughly flat — set stop/target distances proportional to current realized vol. Higher vol also means the dip-entry and rally-sell zones in the price-levels panel will be further from current price — the model expects more movement in both directions, so the actionable levels spread out.
Is the price fair? $64.61 vs FV mean $69.94 (-7.63σ)
What this is Multi-method triangulation of fundamental value: forward P/E vs sector peers, discounted-cash-flow where input quality is sufficient, and recent comparable transactions. The output is a RANGE (not a point estimate) reflecting genuine uncertainty in valuation. The σ figure measures how far today's market price sits above (+) or below (−) the range mean, in units of the range's own standard deviation: ±1σ means "roughly within the normal valuation range"; ±2σ means "meaningfully outside it."
Fair-value range (low — mean — high)$69.00 — $69.94 — $69.94
Current market price$64.61-7.63σ vs FV mean
Methods contributingForward P/E peers, Analyst PT consensus
What this means for you Meaningfully cheap — well below the fair-value mean. Supports new entries (no Layer-3 veto from valuation).
Sanity check — do the two forecast methods agree? all 6 comparisons agree — no haircut
What this is We compute the same forecast (P(target hit first), P(stop hit first), expected value) two completely different ways: random simulation (50,000 Monte Carlo paths) AND solving a deterministic differential equation. The methods make different mathematical assumptions — when they agree, the answer is robust; when they diverge, at least one is being stretched by unusual conditions, so we don't know which to trust. Conviction gets a Layer-2 haircut when they disagree by more than 5.0pp.
Headline All 6 comparisons agree within tolerance — both methods independently arrive at the same answer. No Layer-2 haircut applied to conviction.

Is this stock behaving like its label? ⚠ watchlist B → measured A (looser)
What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.

Swing trade — planned dip → rally Wait for the cheaper entry, sell at the realistic peak — vs the trend-follow path above (buy now, ride the +1σ target)
Derived from the same 50,000 Monte Carlo paths as the Price Levels above, but read for a different question: would patient buying at a dip and planned selling at a rally beat buying at current price? An ACTIONABLE verdict means all four gates pass — dip and rally both meaningful, the dip-then-rally sequence is likely enough to plan around, and the reward per unit risked is acceptable. Otherwise this horizon isn't a swing setup — either trend-follow at current price or stand aside.

What's missing

  • YieldYield (dip→rally) is 3.6% — needs 15.0%+. This is a structural property of the stock's volatility profile: low-vol names rarely produce 15.0%+ swings in the horizon. Would flip only if implied vol rises (gap to close: 11.4 pp).
  • Sequence probabilityP(dip-then-rally) is 27% — needs 30%+. Gap is 3 percentage points. Under pure GBM dynamics, sequence prob is hard to push higher without a real catalyst (earnings bounce, mean-reversion regime, sector rotation). Refresh after the next material event.
  • Reward/RiskR:R is 1.86× — needs 2.0×+. Stop would need to tighten from $481.80 to ~$482.49 (≈+0.1% closer to dip) for R:R to clear. What this is Each ticker has a tier label you set in the watchlist — A mega-cap mature (think NVDA-like), B mid-cap growth (think AMAT-like), C small-cap fat-tailed (think IONQ-like). The pipeline uses your label to pick which statistical assumptions to run. This panel independently measures the stock's actual recent behavior — vol, vol-of-vol, liquidity, history — and tells you whether the label you set still matches reality. If the math has migrated tiers (e.g. a B-anchored name now behaving like C), the conviction confidence gets a Layer-2 haircut.
